                            2. A.    04.19.16 
2.       RECOGNITION 
A.  TSD Mission Moment - Facilities Study Team 
This year, the Troy School District formed a Facilities Study Team to 
conduct a comprehensive review of all buildings in the District. This team 
was made up entirely of members of the community, who volunteered 
and gave countless hours of their time and expertise  to this important 
project. Tonight, we are pleased to recognize the members of the 
Facilities Study Team for their exemplary service . We thank them for 
their thoughtful and skilled analysis and the recommendations they gave 
that will help guide us as the District moves forward.   
We would also like to recognize and thank Gary Wood who was the 
Community Facilitator for the Facilities Study Team.  The District could 
not have undertaken a comprehensive study like this without his 
leadership and direction.    
When I read your name, please come forward and receive a small plaque 
commemorating your service to the Facilities Study Team.  
RECOMMENDED ACTION: Read names and pass out certificates.

                 2. B.    04.19.16 
2.       RECOGNITION 
B. Michigan Reading Educator Award – Natalie Haezebrouck  
Taking the step from a classroom to a reading specialist role takes a master 
teacher and one who has the respect of the administration and staff. This year’s 
co-recipient of the Teacher K -6 Educator Award for the Michigan Reading 
Association, Natalie Haezebrouck, from Leonard Elementary School, took on this 
role with high expectations and vast knowledge. One parent shared that she 
could w atch her child’s confidence and enthusiasm build after developing 
strategies with Ms. Haezebrouck over the course of the year. Natalie developed 
a Guided Reading Academy in her school. This academy brought teachers 
together to explore strategies that would be used in their classroom. Teachers 
viewed videos , and through coaching and shared experiences, helped the 
students at Leonard reach new heights. Her unique strategy was adopted by 
the whole district. Natalie’s actions are a true reflection of the core v alues in 
OneTroy. Congratulations to Natalie Haezebrouck. 
RECOMMENDED ACTION:   Invite Natalie up to receive her certificate.

2. C. 04.19.16 
2. RECOGNITION 
C.  Oakland County Outstanding Teacher of the Year Awards 
The Troy School District has many outstanding teachers on its staff. This 
evening we are proud to recognize the accomplishments of 42 individuals 
who were nominated for  the Troy School District Outstanding Teacher 
Award Program. This is a record number of nominees for the District, and 
says a lot about the incredible depth of our teaching staff. 
The nominees represent exemplary educators at every level and were 
nominated by their peers, by parents and by former and current 
students, all of whom talked about the impact these teachers have on 
the lives of those around them. Their nomination letters described how 
the professionals we honor here tonight go above and beyond to ensure 
that their students learn, grow and thrive.  
Would you please come forward as your name is called? (Di stribute 
certificates/plants.) 
First, we honor 39 outstanding nominees, who have touched the lives of 
countless students and families in the Troy School District. We are grateful 
for their dedication and passion for student success.

President Philippart called the workshop to order at 6:00 p.m.  In addition to President 
Philippart, the following Board members were present:  Mrs. Edmunds, Mrs. Fleming, 
Mr. Hauff, Mr. Schmidt, and Ms. Underwood.  Mr. Miletti was absent.  Administrators in 
attendance were Superintendent Machesky, Mr. Dziatczak, Ms. Griffor, Mr. Harris, Mrs. 
Trotter, and Mr. West.  There were approximately 25 other members of the audience.  
1.  Public Communication* 
Ms. Mary Bogush, 1418 Ottawa, Royal Oak resident, addressed the Board regarding 
the value of the district-owned property known as Section 1 along Square Lake and 
John R roads.   
Ms. Linda Walterneit, 6205 Evanswood, Troy resident, addressed the Board and 
thanked them for overseeing the nature preserve inSection 1 along Square Lake and 
John R roads.  She also talked about the value of the land.

RECOGNITION  
Mission Moment 
Boulan Park Middle School 
group of students and teachers who worked to enhance 
their leadership skills while volunteering their time to help 
younger students find success. The Boulan Park Middle 
students who practice the 7 Habits of Highly Effective Teens.  
They participate in service learning experiences and create 
student-driven Genius Hour projects.   Students volunteer 
weekly at Bemis Elementary and serve as mentors to 
elementary students with iPad activities, math, art, writing, 
science, and reading. Effective Teens volunteers learn how 
to better navigate academic situations and how to plan and 
modify lessons for the youngsters they have in their care. 
They also practice flexibility and differentiation skills. They 
analyze and reflect upon their experience in a student-led 
problem-solving debriefing session afterward.   Genius Hour 
is a student-driven, project-based learning opportunity 
where students spend one to two class periods each week 
researching and working on a project that interests them. 
Genius Hour culminates in student presentations to the 
other Effective Teens classes in the Troy School District at 
the Genius Hour Sharing Event field trip in May.  Teachers 
Mary Typinski and Michelle Slaviero and their students were 
asked to come up and receive recognition and 
congratulations from the Board.     
9
Effective Teens 
At this evening’s meeting the Board recognized a special 
School Effective Teens group is made up of 7 
th  and 8 th  grade 
Sodexo Healthy High School 
Sodexo, to come up and recognize two of our high schools 
for their success with the Healthy High School Challenge.  
The Healthy High School Challenge is a national event aimed 
at promoting healthy lunch and snack options in the 
cafeteria.  High school nutrition programs managed by 
Sodexo around the county competed in this challenge, 
earning points for meals and healthy a-la-carte snacks and 
beverages purchased by students.  The schools who sold the 
healthiest meals, healthy snacks, and beverages during the 
challenge earned points.  The points earned were put 
towards grant money as the prize.   Both Athens and IA-East 
won the competition, earning over 100 points each.  Ms. 
Moran presented the building principals, Dr. Dixon and Mr. 
Brinks, a check for $1,000 to be split between the two 
schools.   Dr. Philippart thanked Sodexo for this award and 
for being partners with the Troy School District.

CURRICULUM 
Schools Closed to Open 
Board of Education adopts a resolution to close Bemis 
Elementary and Schroeder Elementary School as well as 
Athens High and Troy High School to open enrollment for 
the 2016/2017 school year, with the exception of not more 
than 25 incoming ninth grade students who reside in the 
Troy High School attendance area, but wish to attend Athens 
High School.  Students with siblings attending Athens and 
Troy High and currently enrolled in grades 9 – 11 will be 
allowed to open enroll to remain for the 2016/2017 school 
year.

No: 0 
Absent:        Edmunds 
Absent:        Hauff

Report on Schools of Choice 
Learning provided a brief report to the Board for 
consideration of the Schools of Choice Recommendations for 
the 2016-2017 school year.  He explained that many factors 
were taken into consideration in order to make this 
recommendation such as:  current enrollment, enrollment 
projections, patterns and trends, numbers and percentages 
of Schools of Choice students currently in the district, 
projected number of Schools of Choice siblings who would 
be age-eligible to enter the district in 2016-2017, available

Oakland County residents – section 105c).

recommend having them approve Bill’s proposals at the April 19 th Board of Education Meeting.  The

Re: Due Diligence/ Marketing   
18 acres North of Big Beaver and west of John R ( Section 23) 
Dear Mr West: 
Please find attached our proposal for due diligence and marketing for Section 23. 
I. Scope Of The Project 
II. Compensation 
I.  SCOPE OF THE PROJECT 
Great Northern Consulting Group shall provide the following services on behalf of 
The Troy School District. 
PHASE II:  PLANNING AND DUE DILIGENCE 
· Complete approved due diligence 
· Work with Nowak and Fraus to obtain final Wetland Determination  
· Work with Nowak and Fraus to have open space plan created for the RFP. 
PHASE III: MARKETING    
· Determine method of Sale/Lease RFP   
· Create marketing plan/mailing list  
· Determine deadlines if any 
· Create marketing package and duplicate for distribution 
· Create e-package for distribution 
· Post RFP Package on District Web Site  
· Distribution of marketing packages / via hard copies, CD-ROMs, and e-
130
packages  
· Prepare decision Matrix 
· Make recommendations to District 
PHASE IV:  HOURLY CONSULTING  
· After initial marketing effort Great Northern will provide the following ser-
vices on an hourly basis approved by District.  
· Meetings with Qualified Purchasers  
· Assist Purchasers with Municipal approvals if necessary 
· Assist in negotiations  
· Attend Board meetings 
II. COMPENSATION: 
Great Northern Consulting Group will provide the services listed above on an 
hourly fee basis. Troy School District will be billed hourly on a monthly basis at a 
rate of $185.00 per hour for Principal time and $125.00 per hour for support per-
sonnel. The monthly fee does not include engineering, Title Work, Environmental 
studies, advertisements, or printing and postage costs. However the scope of such 
work is at the discretion of the School District. The School District must approve 
any and all of these costs in advance in writing. These items will be added to the 
monthly invoice. Payment for monthly billing shall be due within 30 days of receipt 
of invoice. 
FEE FOR EACH PHASE: 
.   
· Phase II: Due Diligence: Great Northern will work with Nowak and Fraus to 
complete due diligence.    
· Phase III: Marketing:  If approved by District Great Northern Consulting 
Group will Complete Items Outlined in Phase III of this proposal. This fee 
shall not exceed $15,000.00  
· Phase IV: Hourly Consulting: After initial marketing effort Great Northern 
will perform services outlined in Phase IV as approved by the District on an 
hourly basis. This Fee  shall not exceed $5,000.00 
The marketing packages will reflect the fact that the School District will not pay any 
commission. Brokers must look to their purchaser for any commission. Additionally 
the School District may cancel this agreement with 30 days notice at any time, for 
any reason or no reason, with or without cause.

you may have in the next few days.

B.  CONSIDERATION:   SALE OF PROPERTY – SECTIONS 11 AND 16 
The District engaged Great Northern Consulting Group to perform a pre -
sale investigation of district -owned vacant property.  Administration is 
seeking Board approval to proceed toward th e sale of vacant property in 
Sections 11 and 16. 
 RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Approve the resolution below. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
Moved By: ______________________________ 
Supported By: ______________________________ 
Be it therefore resolved  that the Troy B oard of Education authorize administration 
to accept the proposal from Great Northern Consulting Group for Sections 11 and 16 to 
proceed toward the sale of the se District-owned vacant properties as listed in the 
proposal for an amount not to exceed $ 34,500.00. Additionally, the Board of Education 
authorizes expenditures not to exceed $51,200 for due diligence work including but 
not limited to, application for a formal Department of Environmental Quality (DEQ) 
wetland determination and site walk with DEQ, formal topographic survey with 
wetlands and concept plans for the request for proposal (RFP) and that these amounts 
be approved as an expenditure of the Capital Maintenance Fund.

Dear Mr West: 
Please find attached our proposal for the due diligence and marketing for the 2 sites 
listed above. 
I. Scope Of The Project 
II. Compensation 
I.  SCOPE OF THE PROJECT 
Great Northern Consulting Group shall provide the following services on behalf of 
The Troy School District. 
PHASE II:  PLANNING AND DUE DILIGENCE 
· Complete approved due diligence 
· Work with ASTI to complete Formal wetlands determination from DEQ. 
· After Wetlands Determination Work with Engineers to Complete Formal 
Boundary and Topographic Surveys and Concept Plans for The RFP pro-
cess. 
· Prepare report on results of Due Diligence and recommendations for Mar-
keting 
136
PHASE III: MARKETING    
· Determine method of Sale/Lease RFP   
· Create marketing plan/mailing list  
· Determine deadlines if any 
· Create marketing package and duplicate for distribution 
· Create e-package for distribution 
· Post RFP Package on District Web Site  
· Distribution of marketing packages / via hard copies, CD-ROMs, and e-
packages  
· Prepare decision Matrix 
· Make recommendations to District 
PHASE IV:  HOURLY CONSULTING  
· After initial marketing effort Great Northern will provide the following ser-
vices on an hourly basis approved by District.  
· Meetings with Qualified Purchasers  
· Assist Purchasers with Municipal approvals if necessary 
· Assist in negotiations  
· Attend Board meetings 
II. COMPENSATION: 
Great Northern Consulting Group will provide the services listed above on an 
hourly fee basis. Troy School District will be billed hourly on a monthly basis at a 
rate of $185.00 per hour for Principal time and $125.00 per hour for support per-
sonnel. The monthly fee does not include engineering, Title Work, Environmental 
studies, advertisements, or printing and postage costs. However the scope of such 
work is at the discretion of the School District. The School District must approve 
any and all of these costs in advance in writing. These items will be added to the 
monthly invoice. Payment for monthly billing shall be due within 30 days of receipt 
of invoice. 
FEE FOR EACH PHASE: 
· Phase II: Due Diligence: Great Northern Consulting Group will prepare a 
detailed budget for recommended due diligence Items including Great 
Northern Consulting hours required. This must be approved by the District 
in writing.  Great Northern Time is estimated to be $2,500.00 for the two 
sites.  
· Phase III: Marketing:  If approved by District after due diligence completed 
137
Great Northern Consulting Group will Complete Items Outlined in Phase III 
of this proposal. This work cost  shall not exceed  $24,000.00. ( if individual 
sites $15,000 per site) 
· Phase IV: Hourly Consulting: After initial marketing effort Great Northern 
will perform services outlined in Phase IV as approved by the District on an 
hourly basis. This Fee shall not exceed $8,000 ( if individual sites $5,000 per 
property).  
The marketing packages will reflect the fact that the School District will not pay any 
commission. Brokers must look to their purchaser for any commission. Additionally 
the School District may cancel this agreement with 30 days notice at any time, for 
any reason or no reason, with or without cause. 
Thank you for the opportunity to present this proposal for your consideration. If 
the proposal is acceptable please sign below. I will follow up to answer any questions 
you may have in the next few days.
